The JOURNAL OF EDUCATIONAL MEDIA & LIBRARY SCIENCES (JoEMLS),
published by the Tamkang University Press and co-published with the Department of
Information & Library Science (DILS) and Chueh Sheng Memorial Library, was formerly the
Bulletin of Educational Media Science (March 1970 — June 1980) and the Journal of
Educational Media Science (September 1980 — June 1982). In 2015, The JoEMLS is
acknowledged as the first class scholarly journal in Taiwan by Ministry of Science and
Technology (MOST). Since November 2016, the JoEMLS has been changed from quarterly
to a tri-annual journal, published in March, July, and November.

JoEMLS Editorial Policy

The JoEMLS is an Open Access (OA) Dual, double-blind reviewed and international
scholarly journal dedicated to making accessible the results of research across a wide
range of Information & Library-related disciplines. The JoEMLS invites manuscripts
for a professional information & library audience that report empirical, historical, and
philosophical research with implications for librarianship or that explore theoretical
and practical aspects of the field. Peer-reviewed articles are devoted to studies
regarding the field of library science, information science and IT, the book trade and
publishing. Subjects on instructional technology and information communication,
pertaining to librarianship are also appreciated. The JoEMLS encourages
interdisciplinary authorship because, although library science is a distinct discipline,
it is in the mainstream of information science leading to the future of InfoLibrary.

Open Access Policy
With the author's authorization and based on Creative Commons (CC BY-NC 4.0),
readers can immediately obtain full texts for free online via our journal website. We
comply with the DOAJ definition on Open Access, and the Budapest Open Access
Initiative (BOAI) which means we allow and provide. There is no requirement for
readers to register to read the contents of JoEMLS, and JoEMLS does not charge
article processing charge (APC) to contributors and authors.
Archiving Policy

The JoEMLS, as a role of "OA green publisher/journal," provides free access onlined
to all articles and utilizes a form of licensing, similar to Creative Commons Attribution
license, that puts minimal restrictions on the use of JoEMLS's articles. The minimal
restrictions here in the JoEMLS are:
(1) authors can archive both preprint and postprint version, the latter must be on a

non-commercial base;
(2) publisher's PDF version is the most recommend if self-archiving for postprint is

applicable; and
(3) published source must be acknowledged with citation.

EDITORIAL
Diversity, Equity and Inclusion in JoEMLS

The Open Access (OA) policies of scholarly journals have undergone
significant evolution over several decades. Notably, in 2012, attention was
directed toward evaluating the extent of openness in various journal OA policies
("How open is it?"). By 2023, the focus had shifted to advocating for the
assessment of equity in journal publishing practices ("How equitable is it?"). This
decade-long shift reflects the broader emphasis in academic publishing on the
principles of Diversity, Equity, and Inclusion (DEI). The academic publishing sector
has progressed from merely assessing the "degree of openness" to examining
the broader implications of openness, including its impact on journal influence
and reputation, the quality of submitted manuscripts, the protection of authors'
rights, and the sustainability of peer review models. In more recent years,
there has been a concerted effort to integrate DEI principles, with particular
emphasis on addressing equity issues in journal publishing to ensure fair and
inclusive practices.

The influence of DEI has led organizations such as the Council of Science
Editors to propose best practices for fostering DEI in academic publishing.
These recommendations urge publishers to establish transparent accountability
mechanisms and guidelines for DEI, as well as to issue formal statements of
intent to promote diversity and inclusivity within the scientific publishing
ecosystem. Publishers are further encouraged to mitigate potential biases and
discrimination within editorial workflows and to ensure equitable representation
and fairness among editorial boards, peer reviewers, authors, and journal staff.'
The 2023 advocacy for evaluating "How equitable is it?" highlights that equity
concerns extend beyond individual authors to encompass all stakeholders within
the scholarly communication ecosystem, including parent institutions, libraries,
consortia, funders, publishers, and service providers. This initiative seeks to
promote knowledge sharing, address systemic inequities, and explore how
journal payment models, such as article processing charges, influence equitable
participation in scholarship. It also serves as a critical reference for stakeholders
making investment decisions in journal publishing activities.’

* Council of Science Editors, "2.7 Diversity, Equity, and Inclusion in Scholarly Publishing," accessed
March 25, 2025, https://www.councilscienceeditors.org/2-7-diversity--equity--and-inclusion-in-
scholarly-publishing.

? cOAlition S, "How Equitable Is It?: A Draft Framework for Assessing Scholarly Communication
Models of the Axis of Equity," accessed March 25, 2025, https://www.coalition-s.org/wp-content/
uploads/2024/09/HowEquitablelsIt Framework criteria definitions.pdf.

2 Journal of Educational Media & Library Sciences 62 : 1 (2025)

Nonetheless, challenges and debates have inevitably arisen throughout
this developmental process. For instance, do open and equitable publishing
policies inherently conflict with one another? Does granting authors absolute
copyright while limiting publishers' rights constitute "equity"? Furthermore, who
is responsible for safeguarding the "equity" of publishers?

The management of scholarly journal publishing should strive to create
a scholarly environment that is more open, equitable, and inclusive for both
authors and publishers. Such an environment should also foster sustained
attention to DEI principles. Over the years, this journal has implemented
practices aligned with DEI objectives, including long summary in English with
appropriate citations (in Chinese article), dual citation formats (APA and Chicago/
Turabian), and Open Peer Review. Looking ahead, the journal remains committed
to regularly evaluating its policies, refining its practices, and cultivating its
unique culture to identify areas for improvement. This process will involve
setting strategic directions and taking concrete actions to address any potential
inequities or biases.

In this issue (Volume 62, Issue 1), the JoEMLS processed seven submissions,
of which three articles were accepted, resulting in a rejection rate of 5796.
The published articles include: "YouTube University: Redefining Teaching and
Research in Academia" by Bushra Said Issa AL Jamii et al.; "Errata Publishing
in Library and Information Science Journals From 2001 to 2020 and Citation
Patterns of Published Errata: Analysis and Insights" by Yu-Wei Chang and Mei-
Chin Meng; and ^A Case Study on the Information Ethics Gap among College
Students in Taiwan: A Repeated Cross-Sectional Analysis (2008-2021)" by Tao-
Ming Chuang. The journal extends its sincere gratitude to the authors for their
valuable contributions.

Jeong-Yeou Chiu
JoEMLS Editor-in-Chief

Journal of Educational Media & Library Sciences 62 : 1 (2025) : 5-28
DOI:10.6120/JoEMLS.202503_62(1).0039.RS.CE
CC BY-NC 4.0

YouTube University: Redefining Teaching and
Research in Academia

Bushra Said Issa AL Jamii? Amna A’Bdallah Khamis Al Subhi?

*
Mohammed Muneerali Thottoli®

Abstract

Purpose: To examine the effect of YouTube videos (content, accessibility,
analytics, perceptions of YouTube videos, features of YouTube videos, and digital
literacy) on teaching and research among faculties. Design/methodology/
approach: A quantitative research design based on a survey method was used.
The sample was targeted at faculty members engaged in teaching and research
at several colleges and universities in Oman. Partial least squares structural
equation modeling (SEM-PLS) software has been used to analyze the data.
Findings: Digital literacy, perceptions of YouTube videos, and YouTube Video
content significantly impact teaching and research. Features of YouTube videos
have a moderate effect on Teaching and research. However, other factors show
no significant effect on teaching and research. YouTube video content mediates
the relationship between Perceptions on YouTube Videos and teaching research;
and Digital Literacy on YouTube Videos and teaching research. Research
limitations/implications: The study-research approach offers a new perspective
on the intricate ways that faculty use YouTube videos for teaching and research.
This study’s results were derived from a limited sample size of respondents in
the Al-Dhakhlia region of Oman. Practical implications: The study's findings
will improve the dissemination of YouTube videos as self-directed learners for
teaching and research and assist academics, YouTubers, and universities in
better tuning for public education. Originality/value: There is a lack of research
among faculty members; it remains unknown how YouTube videos are used for
research and teaching in Oman. This study is the first of its kind to investigate
how YouTube videos are used in teaching and research.

Research Article

Keywords: YouTube videos, Teaching, Research, Academia, Education

Introduction

YouTube is the most popular social media platform for finding interesting
videos (Kurniawan et al., 2024). With over two billion users and 500 hours of
newly posted videos per hour, YouTube is the second most popular website in the
world (Sui et al., 2022). YouTube videos are largely used by faculty and teachers,
as they prefer YouTube clips more than others for educational purposes. That is
why YouTube is considered the most widespread among social media. YouTube
videos have their advantages, but they also have challenges, including issues with
the video's length, internet connection, and video quality (D'Aquila et al., 2019).
The way the teacher explains the lesson may not be understood in traditional
classes, which has led to a preference for YouTube explanations. The teacher
does not perform a course in a way that attracts YouTube viewers, which may
make them feel bored watching and not benefit, and thus leads to wasting time
(Chiou et al., 2020).

YouTube does not have features such as interaction among faculty
however, it can provide a platform to answer others' questions (Hanif & Saleem,
2023). YouTube systems are affected by phone IDs, fake users, breaches of
confidentiality, and threats. Therefore, threats must be addressed to preserve
caller characteristics using fraudulent accounts and some YouTube detection
tools to enhance the effectiveness of the network (Mahmood et al., 2023).

There is a lack of facilities and infrastructure, proficiency in using
technology, readiness, and lecturers' ability to upload the material that will be
taught and presented via YouTube (Sofya et al., 2022). Age and gender have
a direct impact on the use of technology is reflected in levels of performance
(Cabezas et al., 2017).

The field of study is constantly changing, so lecturers must check that the
content they provide is up-to-date (Fyfield, 2022). Sufficient quality or detail to
understand teaching concepts well so that the concepts can be understood easily
and accurately without the need to search for additional information. Therefore,
there can be a lot of variation in the information provided between videos (Ali et
al., 2022). Teachers are distracted by comments and side content on YouTube,
which affects their explanation and the performance of the lesson well (Yang et
al., 2023). Some videos are not subtitled into the required language, which may
pose an obstacle for those who are not fluent in the original language of the
video (Bitzenbauer et al., 2024). The problem educators have when selecting
appropriate videos is that sometimes the language and substance are impolite
and irrelevant (Fatimah et al., 2023). A few additional issues with YouTube
education, especially in terms of technology, are also apparent (Albahiri &
Alhaj, 2020; Burke et al., 2009). The instructors must make their own decisions

Bushra Said Issa AL Jamii, Amna A'Bdallah Khamis Al Subhi, & Mohamed Muneerali Thottoli: YouTube University ...... 7

about what is appropriate for their classrooms because YouTube does not have
categories that distinguish professional videos.

By promoting research and innovation, Oman's Vision 2040 places a
strong emphasis on equitable and high-quality education through the use of
technology. Instead of using social media sites like Facebook and Twitter to
communicate and express their thoughts, users can like, comment on, and
share YouTube videos. The majority of earlier research was on examining how
potential teachers and students were influenced by YouTube learning videos
(Kusmaryono & Basir, 2024; Yang et al., 2024). Technology is given prominence
in the curriculum at all educational levels in the Omani educational system (Al-
Habsi et al., 2022). However, there is a lack of studies focusing on examining the
effect of YouTube videos on teaching and research among faculties. Using a self-
structured questionnaire, the researchers collected the perceptions of university
and college faculty members in Oman concerning their use of YouTube videos
for research and teaching to determine the trends around the above-mentioned
challenges. Hence the objectives of this study are to examine how YouTube
video content (YVC); YouTube video accessibility (YVAcc); YouTube video analytics
(YVAna); perceptions of YouTube videos (PYV); features of YouTube videos (FYV);
and digital literacy on YouTube videos (DYV) effect on teaching and research (TR)
among faculties.

The remaining sections of this study include a section, review of the
literature, methodology, analysis, discussion, conclusion, limitations, and future
research directions.

Review of Literature

YouTube Videos on Teaching and Research Among Faculties

The YouTube video content signifies that real-time discussions are facilitated
by YouTube videos, including vlogs and live videos, which improve teaching
and research. Sangeetha and Anu (2022) examined at recent developments in
sentiment analysis of YouTube videos, and found that social media and emotions
are important factors in the popularity and dissemination of video content.
The accessibility of YouTube videos ensures its widespread availability, makes
subtitles easier to understand, and supports YouTube access by educational
institutions. Many renowned websites, Asper Rosas-Villena et al. (2015), do not
have sufficient accessibility features for video players, underscoring the need to
make sure they are easy to use. Additionally, teachers can start using YouTube to
improve their professional competency and understand new teaching techniques
(Adu-Marfo et al., 2024). YouTube video analytics includes statistics on the
number of views, the length of the videos, and how many Likes and Dislikes they
have had. To identify video genres and other particular information, effective

8 Journal of Educational Media & Library Sciences 62 : 1 (2025)

video analytics frequently combine variables including dominant color, auditory,
visual quality, and redundancy factors (Sreeja & Kovoor, 2023). Perception of
YouTube videos reflects individual belief that YouTube is a valuable resource for
enhancing knowledge creation, belief in supplemental to traditional learning,
and they have their own technical skills to use the YouTube features. Viewers'
perceptions of YouTube's credibility may be impacted by the language style and
thematic references of its sidebars (Zimmermann & Jucks, 2019). Features of
YouTube videos include various features like the ability to pause, rewind, and
replay, live streaming, real-time interaction features, the ability to change the
speed at which videos play, and other related intended functions. With the use
of a technology called video on demand, users may access previously recorded
video material at any time and pause, slowdown, fast-forward, or replay it as
needed (Kumar et al., 2024). Whereas, digital literacy on YouTube videos ensures
confidence in digital literacy skills to effectively search and find relevant YouTube
videos for teaching and research purposes. When utilizing YouTube as a teaching
tool, Guillén-Gámez et al. (2024) highlighted the significance of behavioral
intention, which has an effect on teaching staff's digital literacy.

Currently, YouTube videos inspired by the classroom are becoming
increasingly popular Thompson (2010). Li Kou and Bonk (2023) aimed to explore
the implementations and perspectives of YouTube content creators, who now
constitute an increasingly important segment of the ecosystem of language
teaching and learning using qualitative and quantitative techniques. Muslem et
al. (2022) investigated the effectiveness of using teaching materials via YouTube
video with peer support for students to master learning the topic, difficulties,
motivations, and also the benefits that are gained in teaching through YouTube.
Maclsaac et al. (2022) aimed to describe methodological innovations in teacher
preparation, so they use YouTube videos in both undergraduate and graduate
courses for prospective university teachers. Da Silva et al. (2023) examined the
quality of information about molar incisor hypo mineralization (HIM) on YouTube
videos.

Li Kou and Bonk (2023) explored the applications and perspectives of
YouTube content creators who now make up an increasingly important segment
of the language teaching and learning ecosystem bu using a mixed infographic
approach was used, combining qualitative and quantitative techniques. Angu
(2019) analyzed the contradictions in the ideas of first-year students about the
use of YouTube videos and tried to examine how conflicting views on the mixed
approach to teaching the components of professional courses and its effect
on the levels of student engagement and motivation during lectures as well as
their overall academic performance. Five videos on electrical engineering topics

Bushra Said Issa AL Jamii, Amna A'Bdallah Khamis Al Subhi, & Mohamed Muneerali Thottoli: YouTube University ...... 9

were shot, uploaded, and then publicly shared on YouTube, where Baron (2022)
investigated which videos might be useful for teaching practical engineering
content in a university context. Technology is a major influence on education.
Many programs have been used to teach students, and YouTube is one of these
programs (Semich & Copper, 2018). It is considered to have a significant impact
on teaching and learning and is used by teachers on a wide scale in classrooms
(Semich & Copper, 2018).

Secilla-Garrido and Hernando (2022) investigated the effects that occur
through the quality of education, the expressive and artistic aspects, and how
they can be accessed easily and quickly and found that Instagram and YouTube
are the most popular means of communication among all. Many accounts
and educational channels were analyzed on both Instagram and YouTube, and
thus their educational quality was determined and all the characteristics of
the accounts and channels were determined by the teachers (Secilla-Garrido &
Hernando, 2022). Instagram and YouTube have a wide variety of educational
accounts of quality in all aspects, in addition to a series of characteristics
common to social media (Rosario et al., 2022). Majid et al. (2012) offer some
suggestions for using YouTube to effectively transfer information knowledge
skills and found that the use of Web 2.0 tools, YouTube, is fast becoming a new
way to teach information literacy skills more interestingly and engagingly. The
widespread use of video lecture recording in universities around the world
presents an opportunity to analyze video viewing patterns in an attempt to
identify and qualify how users interact and learn using videos (McGowan et al.,
2016).

Jia (2019) found that YouTube is becoming an educational tool that improves
teaching and they used a literature review of the journal articles and conference
proceedings to understand the benefits and limitations of YouTube videos in
academia. Users use different methods to learn online, such as YouTube videos,
which have been found to have significant impacts and drawbacks on teaching
music in both online and offline contexts (Waldron, 2013). using YouTube video
teaching materials with peer support led to a significant difference in improving
students’ mastery of subject content compared to those who did not have peer
support (Muslem et al., 2022). Several American courses have been prepared
to direct similar work to other students at the University of Cologne by adding
YouTube videos for students in physics. So the students made short, similar
approximate clops to demonstrate and capture the exact concept at the end of
each session (Maclsaac et al., 2022).

Faye (2014) found that students often turn to YouTube clips, and the
videos created by the lecturer are favorite clips on the internet. Video-based

10 Journal of Educational Media & Library Sciences 62 : 1 (2025)

tutorials have been proposed as an initial solution to address the shortage of
in-person demonstration sessions (Baron, 2022) and suggested that teachers
might consider evaluating their videos using social media analytics, but those
analytics should be critically reviewed and should consist of several metrics that
are measured provisionally. Ali et al. (2022) found that YouTube could be an
educational resource teachers can use to teach young learners and suggested
that it could be a source of information that teachers might use to find materials
relevant to their classes. Similarly, Busaidi et al. (2024) proposed that social
media is one of the ideal tools available to students in Oman for facilitating their
educational experience and that higher education institutions (HEIs) may require
that students use social media to access free and useful learning resources.
While investigating the perceptions of Omani individuals regarding social media
in Oman, Shehata et al. (2023) found that participants have high data literacy to
use digital platforms, as evidenced by their robust verification competencies.
Roza (2021) found that integrating both the Zoom program, which is
considered a model for synchronous learning, and the YouTube program, as a
model for asynchronous learning, leads to effectiveness in micro-teaching and
through lecturers' answers and students' grades that were published via YouTube
video during the Corona epidemic. Whereas Nacak et al. (2020) pointed out that
teachers believe that YouTube clips are a means that is considered educational
material for explaining lessons. Therefore, they found that YouTube is one of the
sites that enhances the continuity of education. Following a thorough review of
the literature with pertinent findings, the authors were convinced to develop the
following hypotheses.

Research Methods

A quantitative research design based on a survey method was used. The
sample was targeted at faculty members engaged in teaching and research
at several colleges and universities in the Ad Dakhlia region, Oman. Using a
convenient sampling metho we finally received 100 valid responses for data
analysis. Partial least squares structural equation modeling (PLS-SEM) has been
used to measure the data and investigate the relationship between variables.
In PLS, a sample size of 100 might be adequate to offer satisfactory power for
moderate effect sizes (i.e., path coefficient of 0.3; Willaby et al., 2015). Similarly,
to ensure the statistical significance of the research findings, Bryman (2016)
points out that a survey's sample size must be at least 100 numbers.

Bushra Said Issa AL Jamii, Amna A'Bdallah Khamis Al Subhi, & Mohamed Muneerali Thottoli: YouTube University ...... 13

Convenient sampling is a non-probabilistic sampling method in which
participants are chosen according to their availability and convenience to the
researcher (Andrade, 2021; Suen et al., 2014). This study has used convenient
sampling to choose faculties who are involved in research and teaching at various
universities in the Ad Dakhlia region, enabling participation from a range of
fields. Convenient sampling is appropriate for employing as long as the sample
size of the target population has minimal impact on the validity of the results
(Andrade, 2021; Suen et al., 2014). In addition to that, convenient sampling is
appropriate for employing as long as the sample size of the target population
has minimal impact on the validity of the results (Yee & San, 2011). During the
data collection phase, researchers took extra precautions to guarantee that the
questionnaires were distributed equally among the faculties. Reminder emails
have been sent to all teaching and research faculty members. The authors
made an effort to get the list of faculties from various university administrations.
The questionnaire was intended to meet the research objectives, which were
limited to teaching and research professionals. The factors influencing the use
of YouTube for teaching and research are determined by looking at the highest
levels of expertise who are engaged in teaching and research in the Aldhakhlia
region of Oman. A questionnaire was issued to collect primary data and examine
the views of experts.

According to Peduzzi et al. (1996), to keep the validity of the model, at
least 10 events for each variable under analysis were preferred. With only seven
variables, 100 responses, and an unknown population, the current study satisfies
the sample size recommendation made by Peduzzi et al. (1996).

Partial least squares structural equation modeling (SEM-PLS) software has
been used to analyze the collected data. Providing specific justifications for the
application of PLS-SEM (such as non-normal data, small sample sizes, theory
building, formative measurements, etc.) is the primary motivation for choosing
this statistical technique (Chin & Newsted, 1999; Hui and Wold, 1982; Usakli &
Kucukergin, 2018).

Reliability and Validity

According to Sekaran and Bougie (2016), the reliability of a test is important
for ensuring consistent measurement across multiple instruments and validating
the degree of error-freeness. In this study, the reliability of the questionnaire was
tested on seven factors, DYV; FYV; PYV; TR; YVAcc; YVAna; and YVC. In a reflective
model, composite reliability is considered a better test for convergent validity
compared to Cronbach's alpha. This is because Cronbach's alpha can either
overestimate or underestimate scale dependability, making it less favorable as a
reliability metric. Composite reliability ranges from O to 1, with 1 representing

14 Journal of Educational Media & Library Sciences 62 : 1 (2025)

ideal predicted dependability. In exploratory work, composite reliabilities should
be at least 0.6 (Rouf & Akhtaruddin, 2018). Table 2 below presents the results
of the reliability tests. Validity is achieved by the consistency of measurements
with ideas about the concept to be measured (Mohd et al., 2021). Convergent
and discriminant validity were linked to the concept to be measured for the
scale used in this research. The instrument's reliability determines the scale's
reliability, and both reliability requirements should be more than 0.70, which
has met the current study. The reliability and validity of the current study are
shown in Table 2. Further analysis was done because the value is » 0.7, which is
acceptable according to George and Mallery (2003).

typically do not raise concerns regarding collinearity issues because VIF values,
which range from 1.283 to 2.775, are far below the maximum threshold of 10.
According to Kwakkye et al. (2018), the structural prototype analysis results
are not adversely affected by collinearity issues, as seen by the low VIF values,
Table 3. Following the establishment of the constructs' reliability and validity,
the suggested hypotheses were examined using the SmartPLS software with
bootstrapping operation.

Results and Discussion

The demographic characteristics of the respondents are given in Table 4
below. The question that was asked was, "How frequently do you use YouTube
for teaching and research purposes?" Respondents said frequently use YouTube
for educational purposes. The majority of respondents (3196) said they use
YouTube every week, while 20% said they use it daily, another 20% said they
use it monthly, and 26% said they use it rarely. Yet another notable finding is
that 396 of respondents said they never use YouTube and prefer other platforms.
Based on the demographic data, 5996 of respondents are men and the majority
of them (59%) are older than 40. Of the responders, 51% have a Ph.D., 32%

Descriptive Statistics

As can be seen from Table 6, the mean and standard deviation, most
respondents chose the scales marked 2 and above, with the mean value for
each item being greater than 2. YouTube variables are effective and consistent
in faculty research and teaching, as indicated by the average mean value and
average level of standard deviation among respondents.

Discriminant Validity Construct

The Heterotrait-Montrait (HTMT) test, which is more rigorous than the
Fornell and Larcker (1981) test, is employed in this study (Henseler et al., 2015).
Table 7 shows, discriminant validity, that the HTMT values lie between 0.05 to 0.89
(much below 1.00), which is clear evidence for discriminant validity.

Root-squared averages (AVE) of all the variables must have a strong
correlation with each other. To ensure the validity of the discriminant, Fornell
and Larcker (1981) pointed out that the square root of each variable in its AVE
had to serve as a comparison link between variables for each other variable. The
discriminant validity of YouTube variables concerning research and teaching is
shown in Table 8 below.

In this study, the R-squared (R?) value for Teaching and Research is 0.620 (R?
adjusted 0.580); for YouTube Video Accessibility, it is 0.384 (R? adjusted 0.371);
for YouTube Video content R? 0.543 (R? adjusted 0.533); and for YouTube Video
Analytics shows R? is 0.320 (R? adjusted 0.305). These values show that the
model is categorized well since this value is greater than 0.30. Finding the impact

Hypothesis Testing

The results of the analysis show (see Table 10, direct effect) that Perceptions
of YouTube Videos (PYV) show a positive impact on TR (p < .001, t = 5.265),
YouTube Video Accessibility (YVAcc) (p < .01, t = 2.747), YouTube Video Content
(YVC) (p < .001, t = 6.217), and YouTube Video Analytics (YVAna) (p < .05, t =
2.313). Digital Literacy on YouTube Videos (DYV) shows there is no impact on
teaching and research (p < .05, t = 1.527) but has significant positive influences
by YouTube Video Accessibility (p < .001, t = 4.010), YouTube Video Content (p
< .01, t = 2.716), and YouTube Video Analyticsa (p « .001, t = 3.762). YouTube
Video Accessibility shows a negative effect on teaching and research (p » .05, t
= 0.281), YouTube Video Content shows a significant positive effect on teaching
and research (p < .01, t = 2.827), and YouTube Video Analytics shows no effect on
teaching and research (p « .05, t 2 0.594). Similarly, Features of YouTube Videos
(FYV) also show no impact on teaching and research (p < .05, t = 0.711).

Discussion
YouTube Videos on Teaching and Research Among Faculties

The results of the study showed that YouTube videos (accessibility, analytics,
perceptions of YouTube videos, features of YouTube videos, and digital literacy)
significantly affect teaching and research. This significant impact on YouTube
research and teaching could be due to the availability of YouTube videos for
research and teaching is widespread, and the inclusion of subtitles in videos
would enhance accessibility for a wider range of researchers and learners. To
increase faculty competence in video production, editing, and content creation,
Vishwakarma et al. (2023) pointed out that timely facilities and workshops are
required.

It is also certainly possible that the researchers and teachers choose which
YouTube videos to watch based on their View Count statistics and how long they
take to watch each video. Based on the number of Likes and Dislikes a YouTube
video obtained, users often select which videos to view. Dienlin and Johannes
(2020) emphasized that a YouTube video's functionality for learning depends
critically on its relevance and quality.

The significant relationship found in the respondents' perceptions of
YouTube videos could potentially be attributed to their belief that these platforms
are a valuable means for augmenting their teaching and research, that they are
a valuable addition to conventional teaching methods, and that they aid in their
understanding of the statistical software used in their research. Tamim (2013)
noted that teachers’ exposure to YouTube videos had a good influence on their
teaching. Similarly, student engagement and learning results can be improved by
using YouTube videos.

YouTube's live streaming and real-time interaction features are useful for
virtual classrooms; the ability to adjust video playback speed on YouTube is a
valuable feature to understand intended concepts; and the ability to pause,
rewind, and replay YouTube videos are potentially the reasons for supporting
YouTube videos' features. Following the pandemic (Covid-19), watching videos
on YouTube has become crucial (Gil-Cordero et al., 2023) for learning. Similarly,
Tahmina (2023) mentioned that research and learning activities are significantly
successful when YouTube videos are visualized.

One important component that can improve research and education is the
digital literacy of YouTube users. This is due to the respondents' confidence in
their ability to use digital literacy to find and search for relevant YouTube videos
for educational purposes; their ability to customize YouTube content to meet
specific learning objectives and student needs; and a belief that using YouTube
videos to enhance research requires digital literacy on the part of faculty

Bushra Said Issa AL Jamii, Amna A'Bdallah Khamis Al Subhi, & Mohamed Muneerali Thottoli: YouTube University ...... 21

members. According to Guillén et al. (2024), YouTube is an effective tool for
educational and research activities that will be helpful if the teachers possess
digital skills.

However, YouTube content does not affect teaching and research. This
is because Vlogs on YouTube are not effective as teaching resources and the
inadequacy of live videos on YouTube hinders real-time discussions. In a study
conducted by Almobarak (2024), YouTube videos were examined to assess the
quality and accessibility of resources which highlighted that the majority of the
videos were in English, which makes a challenge for non-native English speakers.
After examining language accessibility, other developing countries especially
those with sizable populations of non-English speakers, may find it useful to
suggest YouTube videos as additional sources for best practices in both teaching
and research.

Conclusions

This study was conducted to identify the role of YouTube videos in
enhancing teaching and research, by investigating the perceptions of lecturers
in various specializations and evaluating the efficiency of YouTube videos among
lecturers in the Sultanate of Oman. There is a lack of research among faculty
members; it remains unknown how YouTube videos are used for research and
teaching in Oman. This study is the first of its kind to investigate how YouTube
videos are used in teaching and research.

The researchers collected data from targeted experts such as university or
college professors and researchers in the Sultanate of Oman. A self-developed
questionnaire was used to examine the role of YouTube videos in enhancing
teaching and research. The questionnaire gives insights into new theoretical
contributions where the authors mentioned various factors that affect YouTube
videos on teaching and research.

YouTube videos help to assist both lecturers and researchers in various
disciplines, as there are videos that intended users benefit from in enriching
teaching and research for enhancing quality and betterment of self-profile. As
mentioned before, this current study has used a quantitative research method
where the collected data has been analyzed using partial least squares, structural
equation modeling (PLS-SEM), and MS-Excel programs for analysis.

Digital literacy, perceptions of YouTube videos, and YouTube Video content
significantly impact teaching and research. Features of YouTube videos have
a moderate effect on Teaching and research. However, other factors show no
significant effect on teaching and research. YouTube video content mediates the
relationship between Perceptions on YouTube Videos and teaching research; and
Digital Literacy on YouTube Videos and teaching research.

22 Journal of Educational Media & Library Sciences 62 : 1 (2025)

The study's findings will improve the dissemination of YouTube videos as
self-directed learners for teaching and research and assist academics, YouTubers,
and universities in better tuning for public education.

Implications
Theoretical implications

The current study contributes to the body of literature as part of its
theoretical implications. The main research findings shed light on the idea
that various YouTube video attributes, including content, accessibility, and
analytics, impact the results of teaching and research. This conforms with the
Media Richness Theory, according to which Lengel and Daft (1984); Trevino et
al. (1987) contend that more complex media like videos improve the efficacy of
communication. The value of using YouTube videos for teaching and research has
been highlighted in the current study based on the respondents' digital literacy.
Thus, the Technology Acceptance Model (TAM) could be the result. Davis (1989)
proposed that perceived ease of use had an impact on technology acceptance.
The results have confirmed that teachers' use and acceptance of YouTube videos
for teaching purposes could be enhanced by their level of digital literacy. The
significance of participant perception regarding YouTube video engagement for
teaching and research has been underlined by this study. The value addition for
the idea of social learning theory (Bandura, 1969). follows from this, implying
that participants' behaviors may be influenced by their observational learning.
Therefore, a positive impression of YouTube videos and their value for research
and education could encourage individuals to use the platform.

Practical implications

Universities and colleges should take the initiative to offer training and
workshops in video production, editing, and content development to maximize
the talents of their faculty members by making use of YouTube and other related
platforms. For the purpose of teaching and research, this study suggests that
academic staff members use digital media to exchange knowledge and skills,
especially in the field of statistical analysis. HEls can use the highly competent
faculty and researcher's YouTube videos to learn innovative ways to use those
features to create dynamic and interactive learning experiences. HEIs exploring
by integrating YouTube and other digital media into the syllabus might lead to an
enhanced learning experience. By offering top-notch videos, additional features,
and multilingual options for intended users, YouTube channel providers may
engage with educational institutions. By adhering to ethical standards, they can
foster confidence in the ability to lead within the educational setting.

Bushra Said Issa AL Jamii, Amna A'Bdallah Khamis Al Subhi, & Mohamed Muneerali Thottoli: YouTube University ...... 23

Limitations and Future Research Directions

The subjects of the study are faculty members engaged in teaching and
research at several colleges and universities in the Ad Dakhlia region of Oman.
However, this study used convenient sampling method with a relatively small
sample size of 100 respondents, which may limit the generalizability of the
findings. However, this study has been focused only on experts in this research
relevant field. This study explores mediation, and moderation, using quantitative
approaches to deepen understanding of YouTube video's impact on teaching
and research. Future research studies can be focused on using qualitative and
interview methods to understand better. One further issue with this study is
that it is limited to Oman. Future studies, however, could focus on different
locations from a global perspective. Future studies might concentrate solely on
researchers as a cohort to examine their intentions to learn the strategies and
expertise of research delivered via YouTube videos. This study only looked at
YouTube as a source for videos. Nonetheless, additional platforms like LinkedIn,
Facebook, Twitter, Instagram, TikTok, and many more can be used to test the
features for teaching and research. Furthermore, although some of the self-
developed variables with existing literature were employed in this study, future
researchers could incorporate more variables with numerous types of mediation
and moderation statistical tests to further advance the field.

Errata Publishing in Library and Informatio
Science Journals From 2001 to 2020 and
Citation Patterns of Published Errata:
Analysis and Insights"

, a* ces b
Yu-Wei Chang Mei-Chin Meng

Research Article

Abstract

The present study examined the publication of errata notices in library and
information science (LIS) journals, including the reasons for their publication
and citation and whether they were cited accurately. The findings were
compared with those of Yang et al. (2023) to assess the influence of journal
selection on results. According to an analysis of 325 errata notices published in
80 LIS journals indexed in Scopus and Ulrichweb, errata notices were published
as corrections regarding 1. author information, 2. data embedded in tables and
figures, and 3. references, in alignment with Yang et al.'s findings. However,
the proportion of notices addressing these errors was higher in the present
study than in that of Yang et al. (65.2% vs. 44.7%). Among the limited number
of errata notices cited by other authors, most were referenced to provide
background information or contextualize related studies. Most of these authors
cited the original article rather than corrections announced in the errata notice.
The proportion of inaccurate citations to errata notices identified in Yang et al.'s
study (90.3%) was considerably higher than that found in this study (58.8%).
These findings highlight differences in results based on the selection of LIS
journals from different databases, underscoring the necessity of careful journal
selection. Furthermore, the study findings emphasize the need for authors
and journal editors to thoroughly review manuscripts for minor errors before
publication to enhance overall publication quality.

Keywords: Errata, Published reasons, Cited reasons, Library and information science

中 This article presents a new study based on the conference paper by Mei-Chin Meng and
Yu-Wei Chang, which was presented at the 3rd Conference of LIS and Practices (COLISP
2023).

30 Journal of Educational Media & Library Sciences 62 : 1 (2025)

SUMMARY

Introduction

Studies on errata notices published outside of natural science fields are
rare. We identified only two such studies, both focusing on errata notices in
library and information science (LIS) journals (Ajiferuke & Adekannbi, 2020; Yang
et al., 2023). Ajiferuke and Adekannbi (2020) noted that over 70% of errata
notices in their sample addressed errors in author information, figures and
tables, and references. However, their study results did not clarify the reasons
errata notices were issued. By contrast, Yang et al.’s (2023) more in-depth
investigation encompassed authors' reasons for publishing errata notices and
how these notices were cited. The study was based on LIS journals indexed in
Web of Science (WoS) between 2001 and 2020, which raises concerns regarding
the inclusion of non-LIS journals in the dataset. This limitation may affect the
reliability of these authors' findings in addressing research questions specific to
LIS journals.

To address these concerns, the present study employed a robust journal
selection process to examine the characteristics of LIS errata notices and
expanded the dataset by including LIS journals indexed in Scopus. To assess the
effect of journal selection on research findings, our results were compared with
those of Yang et al. (2023), for the same research questions and study period.

The following questions were investigated:

1. How many errata notices were published annually in LIS journals indexed in
Scopus and Ulrichsweb between 2001 and 2020, and what is the proportion of
errata notices relative to the total number of documents published?

2. What is the distribution of the types of documents corrected by errata
notices?

3. Why are errata notices published, how are these notices cited, and are they
cited correctly?

4. What are the differences in research findings between this study, which is
based on LIS errata notices from Scopus, and that of Yang et al. (2023), which
is based on LIS errata notices from WoS? Both studies were compared to
examine the characteristics and implications of errata notice publication in LIS
journals.

Method
Data Collection
The present study defined LIS errata notices as those published in LIS
journals. Bibliographic records of 768 LIS errata notices were collected from the
Scopus database. The following search parameters were used: "Erratum" for

Chang & Meng: Errata Publishing in Library and Information Science Journals From 2001 to 2020 and Citation Patterns of Published Errata 31

document type, "Journal" for source type, "English" for language, and “2001-
2020" for publication years. Errata notices from some non-LIS journals were
identified during examination of bibliographic records; thus, we refined our
definition of LIS journals by limiting the sample to those indexed in both Scopus
and Ulrichsweb. After 443 errata notices from 35 non-LIS journals were excluded,
a total of 325 errata notices from 80 LIS journals were analyzed. Among these 80
journals, 29 were also included in the study by Yang et al. (2023).

Notably, Scopus and WoS may not index all errata notices published by a
given LIS journal. Consequently, discrepancies in the number of errata notices
from the same LIS journals in a given year were observed. To assess how errata
notices were cited, we collected records of paperscited LIS errata noticesbefore.

Data Processing

One author of the present study identified the reason each errata
notice was published by reviewing its content. The unit of analysis was a
single correction reported in an errata notice. If a notice addressed multiple
corrections, the reason for each was analyzed separately. Additionally, whether
the prevalence of errata notices varied based on the document type assigned by
Scopus was also examined. However, Scopus's “Review” classification includes
review articles, book reviews, and other document types, which differs from
the "Review Article" classification in WoS. To facilitate comparison between
our results and those of Yang et al. (2023), each paper with an associated errata
notice categorized as "Review" in Scopus was examined further and reclassified
into the category "review articles" or "book reviews."

Results
The main findings of the comparison between our results and those of Yang
et al. (2023) are as follows:

1. Proportion of errata notices: During the study period (2001-2020), errata
notices accounted for only 0.13% to 0.69% of all documents published
annually, which is consistent with prior findings outside the field of LIS that
errata notices constitute a very small proportion of publications.

2. Types of documents corrected with errata notices: Documents of all types
that are published in academic journals may require correction through errata
notices. Most errata notices were found to correct errors in documents
classified as articles (66.596 in our study vs. 61.396 in that of Yang et al.),
consistent with the findings of other studies and overall predominance of this
document type in academic journals. Our analysis also highlights differences
in document type between Scopus and WoS.

3. Reasons for errata notice publication: Twelve reasons for errata notice

32 Journal of Educational Media & Library Sciences 62 : 1 (2025)

publication were identified, the three most common of which were consistent
with those found by Yang et al. (2023). The most common reason was
correction of author information (including name, institutional affiliation, and
contact details), followed by corrections to figures and tables or references.

4. Citation of errata notices: Only 6.496 (21) of the errata notices in our sample
were cited by other studies, each receiving between one and eight citations.
A likely reason for the low citation rate is that errata notices typically address
minor errors not referenced in subsequent research, such as incorrect author
names or references. Nevertheless, the existence of citations to errata notices
in several studies suggests their value in providing accurate information for
reference.

5. Incorrect citation of errata notices: Both our study and that of Yang et al.
identified a high proportion of errata notices cited incorrectly. In total, 58.396
of citations for errata notices in the former and 90.396 in the latter referred
to content from the original article rather than the notice. These findings
indicate a need to reduce the incorrect citation of errata notices.

6. Reasons for errata notice citation: Twelve reasons for errata notice citation
were identified, excluding several notices with no determinable reason. Errata
notices were primarily cited to provide background information (52.996) or to
support the literature review (25.596), research method (13.796), or results/
conclusions (3.996). In Yang et al.’s study, providing background information
was also the main reason identified (52.996), but the next most common
reasons were supporting research methods (13.396), discussion (12.596), and
literature review (11.796). These differences may stem from differences in the
structure of the documents analyzed between both studies.

Conclusion

The present study examined errata notices published in LIS journals over
a 20-year period (2001-2020), confirming some findings of the study by Yang et
al. (2023) and extending the research scope to provide more detailed results. A
comparison of our findings with those of Yang et al. underscored the necessity of
selecting appropriate journals for representativeness in analyses of errata notice
characteristics within a given field. Additionally, we clarified differences in the
scope of journals and documents between Scopus and WoS and examined the
influence of database and journal selection on research outcomes. These insights
can guide future studies that use data from Scopus and WoS. Furthermore, we
recommend that authors carefully review their manuscripts to eliminate errors
and dedicate sufficient time to the approval process before publication. Journal
editors should also allow adequate time for this process, which is essential for
improving overall publication quality.

旺 勘 误 文 章 出 版 原因

出 版 勘误 文章 是 为 了 修正 已 出 版 文章 的 错误 ，Ajiferuke 与 Adekannbi
(2020) 以 WoS 资 料 库 於 1996 年 至 2016 年 间 出 版 的 356 篇 图 书 资 讯 学 期 刊 勘误
SCH RGN > RRS CE 485 个 勘误 处 进行 勘误 文章 出 版 原因 分 析 ，
惟 研究 结果 仅 指 出 超过 七 成 的 勘误 文章 出 版 原因 是 修正 如 作者 姓名 、 顺 序 、 研
完 机 构 等 作者 资 讯 错 误 、 参 考 文献 琉 漏 ， 或 是 图 表 数 字 误 植 ， 逆 未 说 明 完 整 的
勘误 原因 及 各 勘误 原因 的 文章 数量 。

三 、 研 究 方法

本 研究 共 揉 用 三 种 研究 方法 探讨 图书 资讯 学 期 刊 勤 误 文 章 出 版 原因 及 被 引
用 原因 “， 分 别 以 书目 计量 法 分 析 勤 误 文章 的 书目 资料 ， 上 腔 解 勘误 文章 之 每 年 出
版 数量 及 期 刊 来 源 ; 以 内 容 分 析 法 从 勘误 文章 及 其 已 出 版 文献 的 全 文 确认 出 版
原因 ; 疗 以 引用 情境 分 析 法 从 勘误 文章 、 引 用 勘误 文章 之 文献 内 容 ， 探 完 勘 误
文章 之 被 引用 原因 ， 以 及 加 入 检 栅 勘误 文章 之 原 出 版 文献 内 容 ， 探 讨 勘 误 文 章
是 否 被 正确 引用 。 以 下 依 序 讲 明 资料 厚 集 、 资 料 处 理 及 分 析 方 式 。

勘误 文章 主要 涉及 结果 数据 的 更 正 ， 过 类 更 正 可 能 对 原 出 版 文章 的 结论 产生 影
警 ， 因 此 更 容易 被 其 他 研究 者 引用 。

不 一 问题 ， 排 除 收 儿 卷 期 范 转 问题 ， 造 成 文献 数量 差 界 之 主因 为 收录 文献 类 型
其 办 及 文献 类 型 判定 不 一 致 。 ARE aa A SE | HZ Scopus e Wos parlé ie e e ACE
之 卷 期 出 版 之 期 刊 文献 ， 发 现 被 WoS 资料 亩 收录 之 新 闻 及 个 人 生平 资料 两 种 类
型 文献 ， 闻 未 被 Scopus 资 料 库 收 朱 ， 另 外 也 发 现 相 同文 献 在 两 个 资料 库 忆 文献 类
型 不 一 致 问题 ， 此 是 相关 研究 已 经 囊 实 之 问题 (Donner 2017; Mokhnacheva, 2023 ) 。
综合 上 述 ， 显 示 本 研究 与 Yang 等 (2023 ) 研究 在 资料 来 源 有 相当 差 里 ， 故 圣 於 研究
结果 之 数据 ， 主 要 聚焦 在 研究 结果 的 意 沽 ， 非 特定 研究 数据 之 数字 差 加 。

C fi

AEEER. ERA PARAL EEKE ^ FORRES ^ 会 发 现 以
下 结论 :

1. 本 研究 之 勘误 文章 每 年 出 版 比例 介 於 0.13% 至 0.44%， 类 似 Yang 等 (2023)
研究 之 0.18% 至 0.569%， 显 示 图 书 资 讯 学 期 刊 勤 误 文章 的 出 版 量 在 全 部 各 种 文献
量 中 所 占 比 例 很 低 ， 此 种 情形 也 熏 其 他 学 科 之 研究 结果 类 似 (Hubbard, 2010;
Royle & Waugh, 2004) )， 指 出 勘误 文章 之 出 版 很 欢 消 失 ， 即 使 是 国际 能 见 度 、 影
变 力 较 高 的 SSCI 收 俏 之 图 书 资 讯 学 期 刊 ， 其 勤 误 文 章 比 例 东 未 低 於 SSCI 以 外 之
图 书 人 资讯 学 期 刊 。 基 於 稿件 校对 需要 仔细 检 枫 ， 以 及 有 些 期 刊 仅 给 予 两 个 工作
进行 校对 ， 在 时 间 不 及 不 理 情形 下 ， 作 者 不 可 能 仔细 校对 ， 故 建议 期 刊 出 版
社 给 予 作者 更 充裕 的 校对 时 间 。

2. 期 刊 文献 包含 多 种 类 型 ， 杭 期 刊 而 界 ， 而 各 种 文献 类 型 都 可 能 出 更 勘
误 ， 即 使 多 数 文 献 类 型 之 数量 有 限 。 但 基本 上 “， 出 版 数量 相对 较 多 的 文献 类 型
文献 ， 其 勘误 文章 数量 也 较 多 ， 进 而 所 占 比 例 也 会 较 高 。 由於 研究 文章 筷 学 术
期 刊 忆 最 主要 出 版 的 文献 类 型 ， 因 此 研究 结果 在 预期 范围 内 ， 指 出 勘误 文章
主要 是 修正 研究 文章 之 错误 ， 此 也 熏 过 往 研 究 结 果 是 一 致 的 (Akhaddar, 2021;
Hauptman et al., 2014; Vural et al., 2022 )， 至 於 其 他 文献 类 型 之 勘误 比例 ， 发 更
本 人 研究 所 勘误 之 评论 文章 比例 明显 高 於 Yang 等 人 研究 ， 而 Yang 等 人 研究 是 所
勘误 之 书评 比例 明显 高 於 本 研究 ， 此 主要 是 受到 期 刊 样本 影 嘱 ， 因 六 非 每 个
期 刊 都 会 出 版 评论 型 之 文献 或 其 他 类 型 文献 。 如 前 壕 提 到 Scopus 资料 的 文献 类
型 Review 包 含 多 个 相当 於 WoS 的 评论 性 文章 、 书 评 、 书 目 ， 以 及 其 他 种 类 之
araa o IES > IRAIA Scopus AHERE TE > AEPA SAAS
RA > DARFA SCRA RIS IS E I SOS — EE °

张 郁 前 、 蒙 美金 : 2001-2020 年 图 书 资 讯 学 期 刊 勘 误 文 章 之 出 版 及 被 引用 原因 之 分 析 与 见解 57

3. 二 个 研究 有 相同 之 三 大 勘误 文章 出 版 原因 ， 最 常见 的 出 版 原因 上 皆 是 更 正
作者 资讯 ， 其 次 是 修正 图 表 标 题 、 顺 序 或 内 容 数 据 ， 第 三 是 修改 参考 文献 格
式 ， 此 结果 类 似 Hauptman 等 (2014 ) 在 医学 期 刊 中 人 发现， 勘误 文章 主要 是 修正
图 表 数 据 (31.7% ) 和 作者 资讯 (23.4% )， 但 其 他 研究 的 发 现 则 不 一 致 (Castillo et
al., 2012; Hubbard, 2010; Loepprich, 1973 )， 和 无 法 找 出 不 同学 科 勘 误 文 章 出 版 的 最
主要 原因 。 此 发 现 有 助 於 上 辽 解 图 书 人 资 讯 学 期 刊 勘误 文章 之 主要 出 版 原因 ， 提 供
期 刊 参考 ， 以 能 在 有 限行 政 人 力 情形 下 ， 加 强 检 禹 错误 率 较 高 的 部 分 ， 以 期 提
高 出 版 品质 和 学 入 出 版 的 可 信和 度 。

唯 作者 资讯 的 更 正 又 包含 不 同情 形 ， 除 作者 姓名 拼写 错误 ， 有 了 关 作 者 任
职 机 构 资 讯 更 正 部 分 ， 如 果 不 是 机 构 名 称 拼写 错误 或 不 完整 问题 ， 而 是 涉及 改
以 其 他 机 构 资 讯 替 代 或 新 增 其 他 机 构 资 讯 ， 可 能 涉及 作者 对 任职 机 构 资 讯 的 训
AIZ (Chang, 2023) > 特别 是 作者 完成 研究 的 所 在 机 构 与 文章 出 版 时 所 在 机
构 如 有 不 同 ， 作 者 可 能 是 仅 提供 后 者 机 构 ， 以 能 列 居 服务 於 该 机 构 时 期 之 研究
产 出 ， 又 可 能 是 同时 列 出 两 个 机 构 。 至 於 更 正 合 著 中 作者 序 位 之 问题 ， 吹 然 本
研究 发 现 此 在 作者 资讯 更 正 上 数 最 少 情 形 ， 但 基 於 作者 序 位 所 代表 的 研究 页 献
差 愤 及 对 作者 研究 发 现 意 涵 的 影响 ， 此 情形 可 能 反映 出 合作 中 作者 序 位 的 决定
王 未 在 研究 合作 前 恩 有 过 成 共 巷 或 决定 ， 成 入 共 同 作者 之 问 和 常见 的 委 议 与 纠 粉
(Savchenko & Rosenfeld, 2024) > 故 有 待 注意 作者 序 位 的 更 正 是 否 有 随 图 书 资 读
学 合 著 文 章 比例 增加 而 增加 。

4. 有 被 引用 的 勘误 文章 比例 偏 低 ， 本 研究 325 篇 勘误 文章 中 ， 仅 有 6.4%(21
篇 ) 的 勘误 文章 被 引用 ， 但 此 情形 应 属 合 理 情形 ， 因 必须 注意 许多 勘误 原因 所
产生 之 勘误 文章 丫 不 会 被 他 人 引用 ， 如 仅 是 修正 作者 资讯 或 参考 文献 错误 之 勘
误 文 章 。 而 有 被 引用 之 勘误 文章 ， 每 篇 勘误 文章 之 被 引用 次 数 介 於 一 至 八 次 ，
其 中 被 引用 次 数 介 於 一 至 三 次 的 勘误 文章 ， 其 主要 是 更 正 表格 数据 或 修正 研究
方法 内 容 ， 属 於 较 不 会 影响 原 出 版 文章 之 结论 ; 而 被 引用 四 至 八 次 的 勘误 文章
多 肪 涉及 结果 数据 的 更 正 ， 在 会 影响 原 出 版 文章 结论 之 情形 下 “， 彰 显 出 勘误 文
章 出 版 的 必要 性 ， 以 及 褒 明 更 可 能 被 其 他 研究 者 引用 ， 因 此 被 引用 次 数 较 高 。

只 然 只 有 少数 勘误 文章 被 引用 ， 但 表示 有 研究 者 意 巷 到 勘误 文章 之 存在 与
功能 ， 只 是 本 研究 与 Yang 等 (2023 ) 研究 均 发 现 有 相当 高 比例 之 勘误 文章 引用
六 不 正确 ，Yang 等 人 研究 发 现 引 用 勘误 文章 之 124 篇 文献 中 ， 高 到 90.3% 的 引
用 内 容 是 来 自 原 出 版 文章 ， 而 非 勘误 文章 本 身 ， 而 本 研究 则 是 发 现 有 58.8% 的
错误 引用 ， 人 针对 二 个 研究 都 指出 相当 高 比例 的 引用 不 正确 问题 ， 指 出 了 实际 上
公有 更 少 的 勘误 文章 会 被 引用 ， 因 此 ， 有 被 引用 的 勘误 文章 及 其 被 引用 次 数 都
需要 下 修 ， 也 就 是 勤 误 文章 之 影响 力 被 高 估 “， 节 非 原本 被 引用 次 数 所 反映 出 之
影响 力 。 此 外 ， 此 也 引发 一 个 疑问 ， 错 误 引 用 勤 误 文章 的 作者 “是 否 对 原 出
版 文章 及 其 勘误 文章 之 引用 有 理解 上 的 错误 ， 例 如 认 角 有 被 勘误 的 文章 在 引用

A Case Study on the Information Ethics Gap
among College Students in Taiwan:
A Repeated Cross-Sectional Analysis
(2008-2021)"

Tao-Ming Chuang

Research Article

Abstract

The ethical gap is the personal inconsistency between moral attitudes and
behaviors, which moral education is striving to improve. With the popularity
of information and communication technology, cybercrime has increased and
caused educational institutions to note the necessity for information ethics
education. In 2001, the new Curriculum Guidelines of Basic Education in
Taiwan added the course outline in information ethics to educate elementary
and high school students on the correct use of information. Information ethics
education in basic education has been in place for a long time. What was the
attitude of college students toward information ethics norms? What was the
actual information behavior of college students? Were there any information
ethics gaps for college students? The purpose of the study was to analyze the
inconsistency between college students' attitudes and behaviors in information
ethics, regarding intellectual property rights, privacy protection and information
security, computer use, and academic ethics. A repeated cross-sectional survey
was conducted in the 2008, 2012, 2016, and 2020 academic years to evaluate
the effectiveness of information ethics education in Taiwan. The research
results revealed that although there were information ethics gaps between
college students' attitudes and behaviors, the gap was gradually shrinking
because of the improvement in students’ ethical behaviors. The privacy
protection and information security ethics gap was reduced most significantly in
the four information ethics norms.

Keywords: Information ethical gap, Intellectual property, Privacy protection,
Computer use, Academic ethics

中 The findings of the first survey conducted in the 97th academic year were presented on
November 21, 2008, at the 1st ICPE International Conference (International Conference

on Information Capital, Property, and Ethics (ICPE) — 2008).
Associate Professor, Department of Information and Communications, Shih Hsin
University, Taipei, Taiwan
E-mail: tmchuang@mail.shu.edu.tw

Please visit JoEMLS website to read the Peer Review Report (Open Point) and Article Summary
(InSight Point) of the article.
2024/12/05 received; 2025/03/02 revised; 2025/03/06 accepted

64 Journal of Educational Media & Library Sciences 62 : 1 (2025)

SUMMARY

Introduction

Chinese societies traditionally emphasize moral education by integrating it
into elementary school curricula to establish students' ability to distinguish right
from wrong. However, as students grow up and face various temptations, the
ethical gap—the personal inconsistency between moral attitudes and behaviors—
becomes a common phenomenon in personal moral life. In Taiwan, the
advancement of information education has provided students with widespread
internet access, but it has also led to an increase in cybercrimes, internet
addiction, and information ethics issues among young people. To address
students' ethical gaps, the Ministry of Education has incorporated information
literacy and information ethics into the curriculum for many years. In this paper,
surveys of college students' ethical gaps, which have been conducted every
four years since 2008, analyzed the gap between students' ethical attitudes and
actual behaviors in terms of intellectual property rights, privacy and information
security, campus computer use, and academic ethics.

Literature Review

In the twenty-first century, many countries have integrated information
education into their national curricula to enhance citizens' information
competitiveness and establish a foundation for lifelong learning. In Taiwan, the
Ministry of Education introduced the "Use of Technology and Information Skills"
integrated curriculum to improve students' correct use of information. However,
as the internet became widely accessible, there was increasing concern about
young people's delinquent internet behaviors, such as online fraud, identity
theft, and internet addiction. The Taiwan Police Bulletin Statistics showed that
a significant percentage of cybercriminals were students aged 18-23 (Police
Department, Ministry of the Interior, 2023). The Ministry of Education of Taiwan
was aware of the need to enforce laws to improve students' information ethics.

Research on college students' information ethics revealed a gap between
ethical attitudes and behaviors, particularly in intellectual property issues.
Studies have shown that students' attitudes toward online piracy were influenced
by their level of ethical awareness, especially among those unaware of the
implications of software piracy (Chuang et al., 2011; Yin, 2014). Similar cases were
found in environmental and educational ethics. Young individuals and novice
professionals without experience or training often fail to align their ethical beliefs
with their actions (Aschemann-Witzel & Niebuhr, 2014; Branstetter & Handelsman,
2000). This phenomenon also emerged in various professions, such as psychology
and journalism, and impacted public trust. These findings highlight the importance

Chuang: A Case Study on the Information Ethics Gap among College Students in Taiwan 65

of strengthening professional ethics education and training to reduce ethical gaps
in both academic and professional settings (Li et al., 2012; Mellado et al., 2020).

Method

This study employed a repeated cross-sectional survey method, conducting
a questionnaire survey every four years to observe long-term changes in
Taiwanese college students' information ethics gap (IEG), defined as the
difference between students' ethical attitudes (EA) and ethical behaviors (EB),
and to measure IEG using gap analysis. The hypothesis assumed no significant
difference between EA and EB and was tested using paired t-tests. A significant
positive t-value indicates that ethical commitment is stronger than practice, while
a negative t-value suggests that commitment is weaker than action. The study
framework and questionnaire based on Mason's (1986) PAPA four dimensions
of information ethics (Privacy, Accuracy, Property, and Accessibility) redefined
intellectual property rights, privacy and information security, campus computer
use, and academic ethics. The questions were informed by regulations produced
by Taiwan's Ministry of Education and Shih Hsin University. The questionnaire on
each dimension consisted of two parts: ethical attitudes and ethical behaviors.
The attitude and behavior sections comprised 15 and 20 questions, respectively.
The survey used a Likert five-point scale, ranging from 5 "strongly agree" to 1
"strongly disagree."

The study conducted four surveys in the 2008, 2012, 2016, and 2020
academic years, and the survey subjects were students from the Department of
Information and Communication, Shih Hsin University. The four surveys from 2008
to 2020 had response rates of 75.196, 85.796, 75.196, and 82.3%, respectively. A
total of 974 students participated, with 774 valid responses (79.596). Cronbach's
a reliability tests showed excellent (0.947) reliability for ethical attitude questions
and good (0.812) reliability for ethical behavior questions, maintaining acceptable
reliability across all surveys.

Results

This study analyzed information ethics among college students, focusing on
intellectual property, privacy and information security, campus computer use,
and academic ethics. Regarding intellectual property, students showed support
for ethical norms, with average ratings near 4.0. However, behaviors such as
avoiding illegal software use and sharing copyrighted content showed lower
compliance, with averages below 3.0. Paired t-tests revealed significant gaps
between students' ethical attitudes and behaviors across four academic years,
indicating a noticeable ethical gap in intellectual property, although the gap
showed signs of narrowing over time.

66 Journal of Educational Media & Library Sciences 62 : 1 (2025)

According to students' ethics on privacy and information security based on
four attitude and five behavior questions, students valued privacy and security
highly, with all attitude scores above 4.0. Behavioral compliance was also
relatively strong, with scores above 3.0. Paired t-tests from the 2008 to 2020
academic years showed a narrowing ethical gap, with no significant difference by
2020. This reduction was due to improved ethical behavior rather than a decline
in ethical attitudes, suggesting that students actively enhanced their privacy and
security practices, narrowing the gap between ethical support and compliance.

This study analyzed college students' ethical gaps in campus computer
usage, covering five attitude and five behavior questions. Students strongly
supported ethical guidelines on maintaining cleanliness, quietness, and proper
computer operation, with scores above 4.0. However, behaviors such as not
occupying seats, eating, or playing games in computer labs showed lower
compliance. The paired t-tests from the 2008 to 2020 academic years revealed
a significant ethical gap, with support for attitudes generally higher than actual
behaviors. This information ethical gap, especially in terms of seat occupation,
eating, and gaming, suggests a need for further advocacy and habit formation in
students' adherence to these guidelines.

To assess the academic ethics of college students, the study analyzed two
ethical guidelines and five behavioral questions. Students strongly supported
academic ethics, with high scores for integrity in exams and adherence to
academic creation standards. However, behaviors such as proper citation and
avoidance of plagiarism showed lower compliance. Paired t-tests from the 2008
to 2020 academic years revealed significant gaps between ethical guidelines and
actual behaviors although this gap gradually narrowed. The results suggest that
while students strongly support academic ethics, improving practical behaviors,
such as citation skills and avoidance of plagiarism, were priorities.

This study compared students' attitudes and behaviors in four areas of
information ethics: intellectual property, privacy and information security,
campus computer usage, and academic ethics. Academic ethics had the highest
average score (M = 4.24), followed by privacy and information security (M = 4.15),
campus computer usage (M = 4.04), and intellectual property (M = 3.96). While
attitudes toward ethical norms remained high, behaviors showed an increasing
improvement trend, with privacy and security behaviors showing the fastest
rise. Based on four-time surveys, students' compliance with ethical behavior
has improved significantly over the years, indicating a growing awareness and
practice of information ethics.

The IEG value reflects students' progress or regression in information
ethics. A larger IEG value, representing a greater disparity between attitude and

Chuang: A Case Study on the Information Ethics Gap among College Students in Taiwan 67

behavior, signifies a regression in ethical development. In the study, the privacy
and information security gap (IEG,) was at its highest in the 2008 academic year
(1.46), then decreased rapidly to below 1 by the 2012 academic year. The overall
IEG values for the four years showed a gradual reduction, with a significant drop
to 0.56 in the 2020 academic year (see Table 1 and Figure 1). This change seems
to be linked to the efforts of the Ministry of Education, highlighting its influence
on students' ethical attitudes and behaviors.

Conclusion

This study conducted surveys on college students' information ethics in the
2008, 2012, 2016, and 2020 academic years, focusing on four aspects: intellectual
property, privacy and information security, campus computer use, and academic
ethics. The study systematically analyzed the trend of the gap between students'
information ethical attitudes and behaviors. The results indicated that students
consistently supported academic ethics and privacy/security regulations but

68 Journal of Educational Media & Library Sciences 62 : 1 (2025)

Figure2 The Trend of the Information Ethics Gap

2008 2012 2016 2020

=@-—Information Ethics Gap (IEG)

showed the lowest support for intellectual property regulations. Students’
behaviors reflected higher compliance with privacy/security ethics but weaker
support behavior in terms of intellectual property. The information ethics gap
among students shrank gradually over time, especially in 2020, when there was
no gap in privacy and security behaviors. The study suggests a continued focus
on improving academic citation skills and reducing plagiarism. Despite the high
recognition of campus computer use rules, compliance with restrictive behaviors
such as not reserving seats or consuming food remains insufficient. Intellectual
property ethics also need further attention. For future studies, the research
suggests recruiting a broader sample and updating the questionnaire to reflect
the current web usage environment and students’ practical behavior.

车道 明

偷 理 落差 (ethical gap) 是 人 类 对 道德 承诺 与 行为 实践 之 问 的 差距 ，
反映 个 人 道德 实践 中 1 知 而 未 行 1 的 程度 。 此 现象 也 是 道德 教育 致
力 改善 的 主题 。 随 网 路 科技 普及， 网 路 犯罪 数量 逐渐 增加 ， 教 育
机 构 开 始 关 注资 讯 伦理 教育 重要 性 。 随 台湾 施行 国民 教育 新 课 网
加 强 资 讯 伦理 教育 ， 和 从 小 培育 学 生 资讯 正确 使 用 观念 。 义 务 教育
推动 资讯 伦理 教育 推行 之 有 年 ， 人 台湾 的 大 学 生 对 资讯 伦理 规范 态
度 为 何 ?大 学 生 遵 守 资 讯 伦理 的 行为 又 是 如 何 ? 雨 者 之 问 是 否 存在
资讯 伦理 落差 ?研究 目的 是 对 大 学 生 的 智慧 财产 权 、 隐 私 与 资讯 安
全 、 校 园 电 脑 使 用 与 学 术 偷 理 等 四 项 资讯 伦理 态度 与 行为 差距 进
行 问卷 调查 。 拧 用 重复 横断 面 调查 法 分 别 在 97、101、105 与 109
学 年 进行 重复 性 问卷 调查 ， 以 长 期 观察 资讯 伦理 教育 成 效 。 研 究
结果 烽 示 大 学 生 趴 然 普遍 存在 资讯 偷 理 落差 ， 但 随 大 学 生 遵 守 资
讯 偷 理 行为 提高 ， 促 使 资讯 偷 理 落 差 逐 步 缩减 。 在 四 项 资讯 偷 理
能 度 中 ， 以 隧 私 与 资讯 安全 的 偷 理 落差 缩减 幅度 最 明 竹 。

AR ERICH HER 。

三 、 研 究 方法

本 研究 方法 探 用 重复 横断 面 调 查 (Repeated Cross-sectional Survey )， 以 四 年
局 一 周期 对 不 同 届 次 大 学 生 和 进行 问卷 调查 ， 目 的 是 长 期 观察 测量 台湾 大 学 生 的
资讯 伦理 落差 狗 化 ， 厌 此 间接 回 推 学 生 中 学 时 期 资讯 伦理 教育 落实 成 效 。 横 断
面 调查 有 别 於 纵 贯 性 调查 ， 是 研究 者 在 一 个 特定 时 间 点 ， 以 特定 问题 对 受 试 者
进行 调查 。 过 好 比 在 特定 时 空 对 全 体 受 试 者 拍摄 一 张 照 请 ， 在 不 同时 间 轻 多 次
重复 拍摄 后 ， 将 照 请 影像 世 加 以 是 示 相 片 那些 部 分 产生 变化， 那些 影像 蕉 持 不
鸭 。 重 复 性 横断 面 调查 法 常 被 政府 或 商业 机 构 应 用 在 社会 长 期 研究 ， 以 发 气 社
会 注 爸 中 值得 天 往 的 问题 。 本 研究 针对 特定 系 所 进行 重复 横断 面 调查 ， 全 系 一
次 性 问卷 普查 可 酒 鞭 大 一 到 大 四 学 生 共 四 年 不 同年 代 出 生 学 生 。 研 究 为 避免 不
同年 代 出 生 的 大 学 生 被 重复 调查 ， 而 影响 数据 代表 性 ， 因 而 探 四 年 为 一 周期 进
行 横 断面 调查 ， 调 查 时 间 分 别 汉 在 97、101、105、109 学 年 进行 全 柔 问 卷 普查 。!

办 资讯 伦理 落 产 (IEG ) 统计 分 析

资讯 伦理 落差 (IEG ) 是 个 人 宣称 偷 理 规范 与 遵守 行 角 之 辣 差距 的 数值 。 当
IEG 数 值 较 大 时 “表示 偷 理 落 差 越 大 ， 知 行 不 一 的 现象 更 名 明星 ; SZ IEG RE
越 小 则 是 伦理 落差 越 小 ， 趋向 於 知 与 行 合 一 。 在 四 项 资讯 伦理 落差 值 中 ， 大 学
生 的 隐私 与 资讯 安全 伦理 落差 值 (IEG; ) 在 97 学 年 过 到 最 高 值 1.46， 随 和 后 在 101
学 年 后 迅速 逢 小 至 1 以 下 ， 其 他 三 项 资讯 伦理 落差 值 在 101 与 105 学 年 比 97 学
年 低 ' 但 数值 是 呈现 变动 趋势 ， 到 109 学 年 才 有 显著 缩减 至 1 以 下 (说 见 图 9)。

理 调 查 ， 分 别 从 智慧 财产 权 、 隐 私 与 资讯 安全 、 校 园 电 脑 使 用 及 学 术 偷 理 四 个
面向 ， 对 学 生 资讯 伦理 态度 与 行为 之 问 的 差距 进行 系统 性 分 析 ， 观察 索 积 学 生
资讯 伦理 落差 发 展 趋势 。 从 四 次 问卷 调查 络 果 显示 ， 大 学 生 的 资讯 伦理 规范 误
同 态 度 保持 稳定 不 释 。 从 四 项 资讯 伦理 规范 排序 ， 大 学 生 对 学 术 偷 理 及 隐私 与
资讯 安全 规范 肯定 态度 较 积 极 ， 对 於 智 慧 财 产权 规范 支持 态度 则 是 最 低 。 显示
大 学 生 更 看 重 自 身 利益 的 个 人 偷 理 规范 ， 较 不 关注 稚 访 社会 秩序 的 法 律 规范 。
资讯 伦理 行为 层面 中 ， 学 生 在 隐私 与 资讯 安全 遵守 行为 表现 优 於 其 他 三 项 ， 智
臣 财 产权 实践 行 角 相 对 较 弱 ， 颗 示 学 生 在 不 同 资讯 伦理 实践 上 ， 通常 从 目 身 安
全 的 自 利 原则 出 发 ， 延伸 至 他 律 性 社会 伦理 价值 ， 呈 现价 值 不 均 的 现象 。 此 智
果 与 尹 玫 君 (2003 ) 及 部 佳 落 等 2008 ) 对 国 中 生 与 师范 生 调 查 结 果 类 似 。

育 加 强 ， 资 讯 偷 理 落差 有 逐步 改善 趋势 。 特 别 109 学 年 调查 大 学 生 的 隐私 与 次

安 行 蒿 已 无 仁 讯 丛 理 落差 ， 显 示 大 学 生 为 维 访 自身 网 路 安全 行 裔 也 顾 意 配合 。
迷 到 知行 合 一 的 无 资讯 偷 理 落差 。 此 外 ， 学 生 在 学 术 侩 理 行 裔 改善 方面 也 相当

明显 ' 学 生 对 戴 实 考 武 和 学 术 创 作 正 当 性 有 高 度 认同 驴 度 ， 也 反映 在 近 数 年 对
政治 人 物 的 论文 抄 盐 取得 学 位 反感 度 的 社会 现象 。 合 秆 仍 应 持续 教导 学 生 正确
ER [HERI EE ^ FEIGERIETESETPRETTIS e

大 学 生 在 校园 电脑 使 用 规范 只 然 认 同 度 高 ， 但 在 实践 行为 仍 有 竺 加强, 万
其 是 在 电脑 教室 内 不 估 座 、 不 饮食 以 及 不 使 用 游戏 等 限制 性 规范 遵守 情 沈 较 为
不 足 。 智 正 财 产权 伦理 行 久 相 较 其 他 各 项 ， 学 生 有 较 大 改进 空间 ， 例 如 对 软体
著作 权 的 尊重 与 未 经 授权 内 容 的 使 用 等 。 过 两 项 资讯 伦理 落差 只 人 然 颗 示 学 生 训
同学 校 或 法 律 有 关 规 范 ， 但 实际 行 久 实践 程度 仍 受 因 岗 路 使 用 名 惯 或 校园 正 境
的 影响 ， 存在 明显 的 知行 不 一 的 情况 。

整体 而 言 ， 大 学 生 在 资讯 伦理 规范 重视 程度 维持 稳定 的 趋势 ， 因 大 学 生
偷 理 遵守 行 角逐 步 提升 ， 使 得 大 学 生 资讯 伦理 落差 逐步 籍 小 。 本 研究 属於 个 案
研究 ， 基 於 重复 调查 对 研究 样本 和 与 稳定 回收 率 等 要 求 下 ， 因 此 揉 用 立意 抽样 法
遗 择 单 一 系 所 和 进行 重复 调查 。 研 究 烙 果 和 无 法 普 逼 推 葵 其 他 系 所 或 不 同学 科 。 建
广 未 来 扩大 调查 的 样本 多 样 性 ， 进行 跨 校 或 跨 学 科 领 域 更 广泛 苑 集 数据 进行 调
查 。 此 外 “， 本 研究 使 用 2008 年 设计 问卷 进行 重复 调查 ， 如 今 检视 疝 卷 题目 部 分
已 不 符合 当今 网 路 环境 及 大 学 生 使 用 行 久 ， 若 和 后 乱 进 行 类 似 研 究 ， 建议 对 问卷

题 日 进行 适当 修 亲 。

About Romanized & Translated Notes/References for Original Text

The main purpose of Romanized and Translated Notes (or References) at the end

of English Summary is to assist Western database indexers in identifying and indexing
Chinese citations. This Romanization system for transliterating Chinese cannot be a
substitute for those original notes or references listed with the Chinese manuscript. The
effect of Chinese Romanization for citation remains to be seen.
